## Durable managed experiments

Use the checked-in selected profile for ongoing experiments:

```bash
python3 Robotron/tools/start_managed_training.py \
  --run-dir Robotron/logs/managed-learning-20261015/NEW-RUN --launch
```

Run this from the repository root with a new run directory. Omitting `--launch`
prints the plan. The profile selects 192 actors, batch 4096, n-step 3,
epsilon 0.1, replay cap 8, no C51 target smoothing, and the existing managed
learning-rate control. It records exact environments and systemd user unit
commands in `launch.json`. The trainer and supervised fleet survive the chat
terminal closing. Fleet ownership tags keep automatic restarts from reconciling
private evaluation actors. A soft trainer dependency preserves the fleet during
trainer recovery; isolated systemd failure/restart probes verified the behavior.
Use the manifest's `stop_command` to stop both units; do not
start the generic services alongside a managed run.

Run 26 recovered the last saved model at 227,159,396 learner frames after run 25's
trainer/evaluator disappeared. The protected recovery copy and CPU probe are
under `managed-checkpoints/recovery-227M-20261016.pt` and
`logs/managed-learning-20261015/recovery-227M-cpu-probe.json`. Shared replay rows
survived, but saved replay metadata/priorities lagged; this recovery is not an
exact replay snapshot. The interrupted second 1200-second reference cohort
remains incomplete history and is excluded from new comparisons.

`tools/run_quality_campaign.py` runs a fixed reference/candidate/reference/candidate
sequence on the separate display GPU while training continues on GB300. Its
defaults use fresh 1200-second admission windows, complete game tails, the same
16 private slots, and the compiled FP32 backend. Each leg requires at least 50
complete games and every slot. Both candidate legs must independently exceed
the pooled fresh reference mean by 5% and preserve its median. Plans, checkpoint
copies, hashes, progress and summaries are saved; failed legs stop the campaign
without retries or automatic promotion. This measures frozen policy quality;
it does not isolate a causal learning-knob effect or establish learning speed.

The completed run-26 227M comparison is saved under
`logs/managed-learning-20261015/quality-192M-vs-recovery227M-20261016/`.
All four cohorts were valid. The two reference cohorts pooled 245 games with
mean 281,059 and median 270,100. The same 227M candidate averaged 311,261 over
100 games, then 282,950 over 112 games: +10.75% and +0.67% against that reference.
Both median gates passed, but the second mean missed the fixed +5% gate.
The descriptive pooled candidate gain was +5.42%; pooling does not replace
repeat confirmation. This is insufficient confirmed improvement, not proof of
regression or a plateau. That comparison retained the protected 192M incumbent.

values. It produced no pass result and cleaned up its owned GPU/process/cgroup/
tmpfs state. The original log does not establish whether AMP safely skipped that
attempt. A reviewed diagnostic revision passed 61 CPU tests, then the second

proven AMP skips, no applied updates, unchanged weights/Adam state, and finite
persistent state. It correctly failed its existing three-attempt limit before

is claimed. Failure receipts and exact sources remain preserved separately.
Further numerical diagnosis must inspect gradients before clipping; residual
NaNs after a NaN global norm cannot locate their origin. GPU work requires a
dedicated delegated service and an exclusive display-GPU window outside

state/batch/RNG cases recorded four nonfinite lane-attention parameter gradients
at FP16 scale 65,536 before clipping, and finite gradients at scale one and in

state. Independent CPU verification checked all 80 scientific files, all ten

passed. Receipts are under

writers while capturing. Freshly restarting both branches does not restore

The dashboard's top activity panel shows live learner frames, optimizer samples,
client count, and each cohort's collection/completion state. A draining cohort
waits for its already admitted games to finish; it remains active after the

Chart history keeps scalar metrics and returns at most 2,400 evenly spaced
points over the retained time horizon. Per-client rows and nested diagnostics
remain in the current snapshot. This replaces the previous history response,
which could repeat roughly 43 KB of client rows in each of 40,000 samples and
stall the initial page request. Live polling starts independently of history,
and transient failures retry without closing the dashboard.

The protected 543M CE policy is the current managed reference after both separate
candidate cohorts passed the fresh448M-reference confirmation.
An offline 256-row priority study found CE strongly correlated with
target entropy, but replacing CE with KL also greatly reduced a sampled
importance-weight proxy. The opt-in proposal in `tools/priority_metric_proposal/`
remains unapplied; neither that diagnostic nor lower training loss establishes
better game performance.
The separate source review in
`run-26/priority-analysis/independent-source-calibration-review-20261017.json`
clarifies that the small-sample importance-weight proxy does not estimate a live
batch's gradient scale. Production normalizes within the batch's tree-drawn
PER slice, gives banks its mean weight, and leaves recent/interesting quota
weights at one. Importance weights multiply Bellman CE, while expert auxiliary
losses remain separately weighted. A future KL-priority trial therefore needs
actual batch/mixture calibration and a declared migration of existing CE tree
priorities, insertion hints and historical maxima; the proxy supplies no fixed
learning-rate correction. No priority change has been applied.

`tools/kl_priority_mixture_proposal/DESIGN.txt` specifies the smallest next
calibration after quality release: validate actual batch-4096 sampler mechanics,
then retain eight consecutive executed batches with draw-time origins, weights
and optimizer outcomes. Compare emitted weights, fresh CE weights and fresh KL
weights separately, including their bank coupling, to distinguish stale
priorities from the metric change. Its same-forward five-output prototype remains
unqualified. The separate unchanged-four-output path has now captured actual
CE and inputs for eight live attempts, as recorded above. Reconstructed targets
remain conditional, and coherent matched replay/bank anchors are unvalidated.
Neither observation establishes a learning-speed gain or authorizes a live
priority switch.

The same proposal directory now includes an unapplied sampler-metadata patch
and private fixtures using copies of the actual sampler: 4096-row batches,

weights and default-path byte/RNG parity. All 12 private CPU tests passed after
the quality window was released.
`sampler-preparation-manifest.json` binds the proposed files and original source

unapplied prototype that carries draw metadata through prefetch leases and
records same-forward targets plus actual optimizer exposure outcomes for the

Its 11 protocol and five private CPU boundary tests also passed; actual
whole-training-step coverage then passed all eight tests in the separate

training implementation with real CPU Adam/scaler and synthetic 4096-row,
width-8 fixtures; the CUDA-branch adapters are explicitly test scaffolding.
Current full-model batch-4096 CUDA/AMP/fused-Adam parity remains unvalidated.
The first isolated attempt failed in its compiler-cache storage watcher before
any full training-step attempt or completed warmup proof. Its original exit and

the corresponding terminal archive. The separate watcher revision passed all
25 private tests and completed eight full baseline CUDA attempts using the

The held startup already prevents learning before checkpoint restoration and

and scaler were restored. That same-forward target trace has not been acquired.
The later original-four-output live8 capture preserves the sampler and learning

Future private evaluations can now opt into `--game-boundary-diagnostics`.
The campaign binds the same choice to all four jobs, plans and receipts; the
normal trainer keeps this disabled. It records the previous accepted and
current wire fields and classifier inputs at a game start, plus a later
rejecting first-play frame when needed. Scoring, game IDs and acceptance rules
are unchanged. Validation passed 15 focused tests, 26 journal regressions,
six full-handler OFF/ON wire cases and seven existing measurement/FIFO tests.
The wire tests use synthetic transport and dummy inference; they do not
establish the cause of the earlier 320M invalid game or certify a live campaign.

## Running and stopping

From the `dave` account:

```bash
systemctl --user start robotron-trainer robotron-mame
systemctl --user stop robotron-mame robotron-trainer
journalctl --user -u robotron-trainer -f
journalctl --user -u robotron-mame -f
```

Both services are enabled through Dave's lingering user manager. The MAME
service waits for the trainer socket and supervises its client slots. Initial
trainer startup warms the compiled graphs before starting its worker threads;
inspect the trainer log while this completes. Services restart after failures.

**Managed selection (2026-10-16): 192 headless clients, learner batch 4096**,
after the completed A–B–A throughput comparison documented below. Generic
launcher/service defaults are separate; selected runs set `DQN_BATCH_SIZE=4096`
and retain the measured exact-inference-graph profile. Stop the fleet
service before manually launching another count: `startmame.sh` reconciles the
fleet by restarting existing MAME clients and replacing their supervisor.

For foreground operation from the `Robotron` directory:

```bash
./starttrainer.sh
./startmame.sh --fg
```

Use `./startmame.sh -kill` to stop a manually launched fleet. For a fleet owned
by systemd, stop `robotron-mame` so systemd and the supervisor remain consistent.

## GB300 execution path

Training and inference share the GB300 selected by GPU UUID
`GPU-c0c5d295-6f07-aab5-bbde-b592ea426ec1`. The display GPU is excluded.
Grace's 72 Neoverse-V2 cores run independent MAME processes and the free-threaded
Python actor/replay workers. PyTorch CPU threads default to 8 and interop threads
to 2; `ROBOTRON_CPU_THREADS` and `ROBOTRON_CPU_INTEROP_THREADS` override them.

The launcher defaults to batch **2048**, compiled full training loss, compiled
inference, fused Adam, and replay prefetch. The shared training encoder computes
the joint policy and imitation heads in one attention/grid/trunk pass.
Attention omits unused weights to use PyTorch's SDPA path. Tensor loss reductions
and expert masks stay on the GPU, with diagnostics collected every 20 optimizer
updates. Compilation is warmed on one thread; automatic Inductor graph
capture stays disabled. The managed selection uses separately validated
exact-size actor CUDA graphs; training graphs remain uncaptured.

A bounded **three-slot** pool reuses pinned host minibatches and GPU staging
buffers. A dedicated copy stream overlaps transfers over the Grace/Blackwell
CPU-to-GPU link with training. Separate high-priority inference work uses CUDA
events for weight synchronization and result ordering. Consumer release events
protect both host and GPU storage until outstanding reads complete.

The replay ring remains in Grace CPU memory. Native code compiles locally from
`Scripts/dqn/sumtree.c` on first import and caches the library by source/flags.
Neoverse-V2 detection enables direct FP16-row gathering/conversion to FP32,
avoiding an intermediate FP16 gather array. Native sum-tree parent updates
preserve the existing float64 arithmetic order. Replay locking still protects
sampling and updates; unsupported platforms retain the portable fallback.
Ingestion now applies bounded queue backpressure instead of dropping experience
when faster inference outruns replay insertion.

Overrides for controlled comparisons include `DQN_BATCH_SIZE`,
`DQN_TORCH_COMPILE`, `DQN_FULL_LOSS_COMPILE`, `DQN_REPLAY_PREFETCH`,
`DQN_FUSED_ADAM`, and `DQN_DIAGNOSTIC_INTERVAL`. The launcher supplies defaults
only when an override is absent.

## Learning state and memory

The v28 model retains its four-frame state and existing checkpoint keys.
The migration preserves model weights, optimizer/scaler state, counters, replay,
and banks. The persisted `optimized_samples` counter records logical processed
sample exposure, with schedules expressed in original **512-sample batch equivalents**. Legacy checkpoints initialize it
from their optimizer-step count times 512. LR and expert/BC schedules, target
updates, inference synchronization, and replay-pressure accounting follow this
sample clock, so changing batch size does not abruptly rescale earlier progress.
Epsilon and PER schedules retain their frame-based clocks. Larger batches still
change gradient statistics and should be judged with evaluation results.

Run23 enabled `DQN_LEARNING_EXPOSURE_COUNTERS=1` on 2026-10-16; its code default
is OFF. `/api/now` → `host_pipeline.learning_exposure` and existing CSV
`pipeline_snapshot_json` now distinguish logical processed samples from known
optimizer outcomes and fresh replay admissions. No CSV columns, checkpoint
keys, loss, action, schedule or persistent clock changed. A completed logical
iteration still advances its existing clocks after an AMP-skipped update.
`applied` means an admitted optimizer call, not proof every parameter moved;
`amp_skipped` and `unavailable` remain separate. Pending in-flight attempts can
briefly make the optimizer snapshot unavailable.

Fresh learner replay counts include only normally completed post-nstep DQN and
epsilon rows, with expert/eval/unattributed counts separate; imported replay and
banks are excluded. The prefleet audit verified all six million restored ring
rows contributed zero new admissions. Compare counter deltas only within an
unchanged process/instance/epoch; load/clear/reset invalidates the relevant
baseline. A behavior-change burn-in additionally needs a verified queue/nstep
boundary. Counters and model diagnostics are not an atomic snapshot, so skip
increments are observation brackets rather than per-row causal evidence. Full
scope and API semantics are in `tools/PIPELINE_REPORTING.md`; startup evidence is
`logs/managed-learning-20261015/run-23/startup-readonly-independent-audit.json`.
The first fixed health window (11:38:36.064–11:41:36.078 UTC) recorded 3,151
optimizer outcomes: 3,150 applied, one AMP-skipped and zero unavailable. All
813,040 new learner inserts were DQN/epsilon; expert and eval inserts stayed
separate. The Inf-gradient diagnostic and skip observation brackets are retained.
Its 71,833.77 samples/s (+3.714% versus run22) is descriptive, not causal overhead
or learning-quality evidence. Final guards and original observer-predicate
correction are retained in run23's `exposure-health-final-independent-audit.json`.

The six-million-transition ring stores state/next-state arrays in FP16: about
249 GiB for those arrays plus metadata. `/dev/shm` is configured at **320 GiB**;
capacity is checked and backing files allocated before filling the ring. Only
small minibatch staging buffers are pinned; the entire replay ring is not.
The RAM-backed ring survives a trainer process restart but is lost on reboot.

Checkpoints and persistent banks live in `Robotron/models_dqn`. Model saves use
an atomic temporary-file replacement and keep the previous checkpoint as `.bak`.
Periodic autosaves skip the large replay save; forced/manual saves and orderly
shutdown save its metadata and current ring state. Stop through systemd or the
application's normal exit so it can finish saving; the service allows 180 seconds
for shutdown. The source from before optimization is backed up under
`/home/dave/source/mame-validation/perf-backup`; previous checkpoint `.bak`
files remain beside their models.

Bank field output defaults to `DQN_STREAM_BANK_OUTPUT=0`. The opt-in streaming
route retains coherent bank snapshots, the padded `.npy` format, file/directory
durability steps, and atomic `CURRENT` publication with retained complete
generations. It changes how field files are written. Full field phase counters
are available as `/api/now` → `bank_persistence.output` and CSV
`bank_persistence_json` → `output`; no CSV header change is required. These host
elapsed timers exclude capture/release, metadata and selector/directory work.
Their scope and bounded recent-event retention are documented in
`tools/PIPELINE_REPORTING.md`.

On 2026-10-16, run19's natural memmap event published both banks without bank
write errors, while 34 main inference-deadline errors caused reconnections; all
192 pinned MAME processes survived. Recorded mmap close time was 26.916 ms
total (9.964 ms maximum), so this event does not establish a long unmap stall.
Neither GC/snapshot release nor the cause of the deadline burst is established.
The full observation is in
`logs/managed-learning-20261015/run-19/natural-bank-memmap-independent-audit.json`.
Run20's first natural streaming event on 2026-10-16 published both banks with
zero write errors, inference deadlines or journal disconnects, preserving all
192 actor identities. The earlier memmap event retains its 34 deadline errors.
Observed full capture/write brackets were 32.813 s (memmap) and 33.003 s (stream);
this does not show a faster whole save. Live policy, snapshot payload and
uncontrolled host work differ. This first pair supports continued observation
of the opt-in trial, not a causal speedup or universal reliability fix. See
`logs/managed-learning-20261015/run-20/natural-bank-stream-vs-memmap-independent-comparison.json`.
Two further natural streaming cycles at 10:27 and 10:57 UTC also published
both banks with zero bank/field errors, main deadlines or journal disconnects,
preserving all 192 actor identities through at least 134 seconds of recovery.
The three observed cycles total six bank publications. The recurrence audits
are `natural-bank-repeat-independent-audit.json` and
`natural-bank-third-independent-audit.json` in the same run20 directory. They
strengthen observed recurrence evidence without establishing a causal speedup,
GC/snapshot/unmap cause or universal reliability fix. Published-file checks cover
headers, geometry, sizes and sampled rows, not exhaustive payload equality or
filesystem durability. The flag remains default OFF; the retained trial is ON.

## Python environment and optional WebRTC

The working free-threaded runtime is under
`/home/dave/source/robotron-python/cpython-3.14.8+freethreaded-linux-aarch64-gnu`;
the `cpython-3.14+freethreaded-linux-aarch64-gnu` alias also exists. The older
Python 3.12 environment remains in `.venv` for a foreground fallback:

```bash
ROBOTRON_PYTHON=/home/dave/source/repos/ArcadeAI/.venv/bin/python \
  DQN_BATCH_SIZE=512 DQN_TORCH_COMPILE=0 DQN_FULL_LOSS_COMPILE=0 \
  DQN_REPLAY_PREFETCH=0 DQN_FUSED_ADAM=0 ./starttrainer.sh
```

Stop the normal trainer/fleet services before using that command. This keeps the
same source/checkpoint format while disabling the optional accelerated paths.

For a new environment, the runtime/toolchain sources are Astral's managed
free-threaded CPython and PyTorch's official CUDA 13.0 wheel index. From the repo
root, using the retained `uv` executable:

```bash
export UV_PYTHON_INSTALL_DIR=/home/dave/source/robotron-python
.venv/bin/uv python install 3.14.8t
.venv/bin/uv venv --python 3.14.8t .venv-gb300
.venv/bin/uv pip install --python .venv-gb300/bin/python \
  --index https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu130 'torch==2.13.0+cu130'
```

`requirements-gb300.lock` records the working dependency inventory, including
the local optional SRTP wheel. Retain the working environment when reproducing
this setup: its `nvidia-cusparselt-cu13` distribution contains an upstream SBSA
wheel-metadata tag that `uv pip check` flags, although this installation's Torch
imports and GB300 GPU execution were validated. A clean dependency check for a
new environment has not been established.

Optional WebRTC preview uses aiortc **1.15.0** and PyAV **17.1.0**. The upstream
`pylibsrtp 1.0.0` source was downloaded from official PyPI and SHA256-verified;
its packaging patch preserves the native `cp314-cp314t-linux_aarch64` wheel tag
on free-threaded Python. Conventional CPython retains upstream ABI3 tagging.
The extension/protocol code is unchanged. The retained wheel is:

`tools/pylibsrtp-free-threaded/wheels/pylibsrtp-1.0.0-cp314-cp314t-linux_aarch64.whl`

Install it from the repo root before aiortc:

```bash
.venv/bin/uv pip install --python .venv-gb300/bin/python \
  Robotron/tools/pylibsrtp-free-threaded/wheels/pylibsrtp-1.0.0-cp314-cp314t-linux_aarch64.whl
.venv/bin/uv pip install --python .venv-gb300/bin/python 'aiortc==1.15.0'
```

The sibling provenance JSON, packaging patch, and validation JSON record source
and wheel hashes. Imports retain the disabled GIL; SRTP encryption/decryption,
WebRTC video-offer creation, and PyAV frame conversion passed. Google CRC32C uses
its upstream pure-Python fallback in this environment.

## ROMs and validation

`stage_robotron_romset.sh` stages the patched `robotron.zip` into
`roms/robotron`; checksum warnings for the intentional patch are expected.
The stock blue-label archive was independently validated with ARM64 MAME 0.264.
Working cabinet NVRAM settings were copied from ubvmdell to
`~/.mame/nvram/robotron/nvram`; the original is backed up in
`~/source/mame-validation/nvram-before-initialization`.

Isolated correctness validation covers old/new checkpoint keys, masked expert
loss values/gradients, shared-encoder parameter gradients, and empty/sparse/dense
object bags. CPU prefetch lifecycle tests and a separate GB300 test cover bounded
leases, field precision, pinned storage, event ordering, invalidation, and safe
shutdown. These are correctness checks, not live throughput measurements.
Batch-size benchmarks are isolated from the live fleet. The original batch-512
path measured 4,520 samples/s; compiled shared-encoder batches 512/1024/2048
measured 25,905/45,322/78,038 samples/s. Compiling the complete loss on the
free-threaded runtime reached 84,718 samples/s (18.7x original). These are short
compute benchmarks using a fixed replay subset, synthetic rewards, three warmup
and ten timed updates; compilation startup is excluded.

Final live cohorts used the same running checkpoint and replay, 12 dashboard
observations spaced five seconds apart after a 12-second startup wait:

| Clients | Frames/s | Training samples/s | Median inference ms | Median GPU use |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 16 | 2,129 | 51,881 | 7.38 | 51% |
| 32 | 1,076 | 54,764 | 9.58 | 50.5% |
| 48 | 1,421 | 34,427 | 14.16 | 47.5% |

Choose 16: 32 only improved training about 6% while collecting about half the
frames, and 48 worsened training. These sequential short cohorts are sensitive
to episode mix and replay-bank activity; they establish a practical starting
configuration, not a universal optimum or improved policy score. Replay-drop
counters remained zero. Raw results and earlier comparison stages are under
`~/source/mame-validation/actor-results*.json`; isolated logs are `bench-*.log`.
Grace's direct FP16 gather reduced an alternating-order random-row CPU
microbenchmark from 10.94 to 6.87 ms per state array (1.59x).

The production minimum replay remains **500,000 transitions**. Monitor long-run
EScr1M and evaluation levels to judge learning quality separately from speed.

## Fresh runs and durable learning reports

`tools/start_fresh_console.sh /dev/pts/N` explicitly stops the trainer/fleet,
removes **all** `models_dqn` contents (including settings, best/backup weights,
optimizer state and both demonstration banks), removes the tmpfs replay ring,
and starts a fresh trainer. Choose the current SSH console from `who`; the
script mirrors output there while retaining its own trainer terminal. It does
not start the actor fleet. Once the trainer is listening, launch
`MAME_BIN=/usr/games/mame SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy ./startmame.sh 16 novideo`.

Each process writes `logs/run-<UTC>/training.csv` and `metadata.json`; the fresh
launcher also saves `console.log`. `ROBOTRON_RUN_DIR` can select the directory.
The 30-second console table adds samples/s, mean GPU utilization and watts,
GPU memory MiB, samples/joule, and sampled CUDA compute milliseconds. GPU
telemetry is sampled once a second on the GPU selected by the launcher. Blank
CSV GPU values and `NA` console cells mean unavailable telemetry, not zero use.
Power/energy efficiency refers to the GPU, not the whole machine.

CSV rows retain numeric evaluation scores/levels and evaluation-window counts,
exploration/expert rates, replay and actor mix, rewards, Q/gradient/BC diagnostics,
per-bank losses, inference latency, optimizer/sample counts and timing. The
first row establishes the throughput baseline. CUDA compute time is sampled
at the diagnostic interval using events and the existing readback barrier;
S/X/C/P are host wall timings, and asynchronous transfer/compute work may be
charged to the later readback. They must not be interpreted as separate kernel
timings or added to prefetch sampling time to estimate total step duration.

Fresh runs retain the 500,000-transition learning warmup and the 32 training
samples per incoming frame replay-pressure cap. Both intentionally limit GPU
activity when data is insufficient. Increase utilization only when evaluation
quality and experience refresh support more optimization; GPU utilization alone
is not a measure of learning success. Compare batch sizes using evaluation
score versus wall time and versus optimized samples.

## Live batch-size comparison

`tools/tune_batch.py` preserves learning state while comparing batches 2048,
8192, 4096 and 6144 with 16 actors, then repeats the two leading sizes and
leaves the winner training. Stop any existing trainer before running it. It
owns the local MAME fleet for the comparison. Each restart saves the current
checkpoint, optimizer, banks and replay; no reset script is involved.

Trials discard the first telemetry row and average three subsequent 30-second
intervals with all 16 clients connected. The ranking gives equal proportional
weight to incoming frames/sec and optimized samples/sec (their geometric mean).
GPU utilization, power, inference latency and CUDA compute times are reported
alongside those rates. This is a throughput comparison during continuing
learning, not a controlled evaluation of policy quality. Top-two repeat trials
reduce the influence of changing game states and policy progress.

Reports live under `logs/batch-tuning-<UTC>/`; `logs/batch-tuning/latest.txt`
identifies the current directory. `results.csv`/`results.json` retain trial
measurements, and `selection.json` records the selected batch and continuing
trainer PID. Per-trial directories retain the full numeric and console logs.
The selected value should also be set as the `starttrainer.sh` default after
reviewing the measurements, so normal restarts use it.

The replay-pressure check now runs before the learner fetches a minibatch and
in the prefetch producer's enable condition, as well as at the final training
guard. Previously a capped learner repeatedly gathered/staged minibatches only
to discard them. That unnecessary work held replay locks and competed with actor
traffic. A small number of already in-flight batches remain bounded by the
three-slot pool. The CSV includes cumulative `prefetched_batches` and
`replay_pressure_waits`; once running, batch-count deltas should closely track
optimizer-update deltas. The 32-samples/frame limit and all learning schedules
remain unchanged.

`DQN_DIRECT_REPLAY_FILL=1` opts into sampling states directly into the bounded
pinned staging slots. The default is 0. It skips the ring/bank concatenation and
the additional full-state host copy, preserving sample indices, RNG draws,
quotas, weights, and fp32 bank values. Output validation rejects aliases with
replay storage, metadata, or the priority tree. GPU release events protect slot
refills; the replay ring remains unpinned. CSV fields `direct_state_batches`,
`replay_direct_state_fill`, and `stage_host_ms` expose the actual path and cost.
A bounded CPU comparison at batch 2048/state width 11128 measured 34.78 ms for
the old state gather/concat/stage chain and 11.71 ms for direct filling. This
states-only result excludes the rest of sampling and GPU compute. CPU exactness
tests and a separate GB300 native-to-pinned ownership check passed. Evidence is
under `logs/managed-learning-20261015/replay-gather-comparison.json` and
`replay-direct-cuda-validation.json`; live learning impact is evaluated separately.

### October 15, 2026 batch sweep

After the pressure-gating fix, live trials tested 2048 → 8192 → 4096 → 6144,
then repeated 8192 and 2048. The top-two combined results selected **2048**;
the launcher already defaults to this value and the continuing process uses it.
Latest trials for each size (three 30-second intervals each):

| Batch | Game frames/s | Training samples/s | Inference ms | GPU use |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 2048 (confirmation) | 1766 | 58319 | 7.27 | 53.4% |
| 4096 | 1518 | 56952 | 8.66 | 47.9% |
| 6144 | 1538 | 56123 | 8.49 | 48.5% |
| 8192 (confirmation) | 1578 | 56255 | 9.58 | 45.8% |

All six trials reported zero dropped transitions. The first 2048 trial measured
1947 FPS / 47384 samples/s; the first 8192 trial measured 1546 / 57721. Selection
used both runs of each finalist, rather than only their latest results. Short
sequential live trials contain episode/policy variation and do not establish a
universal optimum or improved learning quality. Full evidence and continuing-run
selection: `logs/batch-tuning-20261015T222325Z/`.

## Managed learning experiments

`models_dqn/learning_control.json` is a validated, atomically updated live
experiment control. It supports an experiment label and an optional epsilon
ramp with `anchor` (persisted learner-frame clock), `start`, `end`, and
`duration`. The main thread refreshes it during training. Invalid controls keep
the last valid schedule. Manual epsilon settings retain their existing priority.
`tools/manage_learning.py status` shows the active experiment and whole-game
statistics; `ramp --label NAME --end 0.5 --duration 2000000` anchors a smooth
change at the current epsilon/learner frame, and `hold --label NAME` retains the
schedule while starting a distinct measurement phase. `checkpoint --label TAG`
requests an atomic model/optimizer/counter snapshot in `managed-checkpoints`.
These controls do not reset replay or the model.
The optional `lr_scale` multiplies the scheduled learning rate (and any manual
keyboard scale), defaults to 1, and persists across restarts in this control
file. It does not reset Adam or re-anchor the cosine schedule. Values outside
0.01..2 and invalid controls retain the last valid setting.

For a fixed-policy comparison, use `measure --label UNIQUE --checkpoint PATH
--cohort 180 --timeout 900`; omit `--checkpoint` to snapshot and measure the
current online model. Every actor runs greedily from fresh wave-1 games. The
learner and replay writes pause, and measurement frames do not advance learner
schedules or grant replay-pressure credit. Admission uses a fixed time window;
all games started in that window must finish, including its long tails. Missing
actors, abandoned games, or a timeout invalidate the whole measurement. The
controller restores the online inference policy and resumes training when the
leg ends. `resume` clears the request early; that leg is recorded as cancelled.
Unique labels prevent an old request from repeating across a trainer restart.
Receipts and exact current-policy snapshots live in
`models_dqn/managed-measurements/`. Only valid receipts support comparisons.

Recovery experiments may override `DQN_N_STEP`, `DQN_MAX_SAMPLES_PER_FRAME`, and
`DQN_C51_TARGET_SMOOTHING` without changing their historical defaults. Changing
n-step length requires fresh replay and banks: stored rewards are already
aggregated, so existing 16-step rows cannot become 3-step targets by relabeling.
Archive the full quiescent ring, banks, model, settings, and control before
starting a fresh lineage. The 500,000-transition diversity gate collects data
before any optimizer work. Warmup frames do not grant later replay-update
credit; the per-run pressure anchor is refreshed while replay is below the
gate. This changes no persistent learner/model clock. The console labels these
phases `WARMUP`, `TRAIN`, and `MEASURE`, and the CSV retains the budget anchors.

The GB300 launcher now defaults to the confirmed recovery settings: n-step 3,
8 samples per incoming fleet frame, C51 target smoothing 0, and direct replay
filling. The persistent control holds epsilon at 0.1 and LR scale at 0.5.
Two valid frozen cohorts of the same saved policy scored 36,770 and 37,031
points (111 and 116 games), versus 30,882 across 120 games for the protected
reference. The pooled descriptive mean is 36,904 (+19.5%). The measured policy
is preserved at `models_dqn/managed-incumbents/fresh3-250k-confirmed.pt`;
`frozen-reference-vs-fresh3-250k-campaign.json` records checkpoint/receipt hashes,
independent game IDs, actor-cluster sensitivity intervals, and statistical limits.
Continuing the same configuration to one million post-warmup learner frames
raised two further frozen cohorts to 43,391 and 43,383 points (90 and 102 games).
Their pooled mean is 43,387 over 192 games, 17.6% above the earlier recovery
policy and 40.5% above the protected reference. That policy is preserved at
`models_dqn/managed-incumbents/fresh3-1M-confirmed.pt`; the full comparison is
`logs/managed-learning-20261015/frozen-fresh3-1M-confirmed-campaign.json`.
At two million post-warmup learner frames, two frozen cohorts scored 46,220 and
50,700 (91 and 126 games). Their pooled mean of 48,822 over 217 games is 12.5%
above the one-million policy and 58.1% above the original reference. That
policy is preserved at `models_dqn/managed-incumbents/fresh3-2M-confirmed.pt`;
`logs/managed-learning-20261015/frozen-fresh3-2M-confirmed-campaign.json` records
the source hashes, saved-policy clocks, and comparison limits. The sensitivity
intervals only narrowly clear parity, so this smaller gain remains provisional.
At four million frames, 86 and 111 complete frozen games scored 56,468 and
58,852. Their pooled mean is 57,811 over 197 games, 18.4% above the two-million
policy and 87.2% above the original reference. That policy is preserved at
`models_dqn/managed-incumbents/fresh3-4M-confirmed.pt`. See
`logs/managed-learning-20261015/frozen-fresh3-4M-confirmed-campaign.json` for exact
checkpoint clocks/hashes, cohort validation, and pacing/RNG/batching caveats:
these evaluations use the validated readiness helper described below.
At eight million frames, 70 and 97 complete frozen games scored 78,823 and
75,684. Their pooled mean is 76,999 over 167 games, 33.2% above the four-million
policy and 149.3% above the original reference. That historical protected policy
is `models_dqn/managed-incumbents/fresh3-8M-confirmed.pt`; the full audit is
`logs/managed-learning-20261015/frozen-fresh3-8M-confirmed-campaign.json`.
The four- and eight-million cohorts used the same GB300 FP32 inference,
16-actor coverage and 1000-microsecond readiness backend; training uses FP16
autocast. Saved-policy clocks and later evaluation-driver clocks are distinct.
This is a combined recovery result, not attribution to an individual knob.
Raw Python configuration defaults remain available for historical runs; resuming
the archived 16-step lineage requires matching explicit environment overrides.

The legacy `EScr1M` metric is cumulative game score at lost-life boundaries,
with a rolling window measured in evaluation playable frames. It is neither
independent full-game score nor a short, nonoverlapping evaluation cohort.
The terminal now labels it `ELife1M`. Independent game outcomes are journaled to
`ROBOTRON_RUN_DIR/games.jsonl`, with life details in `lives.jsonl`. Startup
attachments, disconnects, and games crossing experiment labels are excluded
from comparisons. Small and zero scores have no admission cutoff. The wire has
no explicit game-over bit; boundary reasons record accepted score resets or
confirmed life exhaustion. The console's `EGames`, `EGameScr`, and `EGameSE`
columns and `/api/now`'s `eval_games` object report eligible whole-game counts,
mean and standard error for the current experiment.

Score ingestion tolerates bounded BCD carry dips during uninterrupted, same-wave
live play before applying the broad low-score new-game allowance. It retains
the game ID and prior score baseline, preventing a false final score and an
inflated recovery reward. Real inactive/start/wave-reset edges retain their
existing classification. Coercions are logged with raw scores; game records
also retain their start frame for boundary audits. Strict frozen measurement
still rejects a game whose first playable observation is above wave 1.

Additional C51 diagnostics distinguish target entropy from categorical KL,
record probability mass outside support before projection/clamping, taken-action
edge mass and top-two action Q gap. They are sampled at the existing diagnostic
interval and preserve the objective/gradients. Experiment history, raw snapshots,
audit findings and current management state are under
`logs/managed-learning-20261015/`. The read-only CPU checkpoint probe uses two
threads and at most 512 total sampled replay/bank rows by default.

MAME's original action receive loop busy-polls its TCP socket. The optional
readiness helper changes only a zero-timeout `select` on one connected TCP read
socket whose peer port is 9998. It waits for readiness for at most the selected
interval and returns immediately when data arrives. Lua then uses MAME's host
wall clock for the action deadline and legacy packet settling; emulated time
never advances during the wait. The installed MAME disables Lua dynamic modules,
so the helper is preloaded into each MAME process through a dedicated wrapper:

```bash
ROBOTRON_SOCKET_WAIT_LIBRARY="$(python3 tools/build_socket_wait.py)" \
ROBOTRON_SOCKET_WAIT_US=1000 \
MAME_BIN="$PWD/tools/mame_with_socket_wait.sh" SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy \
./startmame.sh 128 novideo --supervise
```

The wrapper requires an explicit interval of 100–10000 microseconds and applies
`LD_PRELOAD` only when executing MAME, including supervisor respawns. The normal
launcher remains opt-in and retains its original behavior without the wrapper.
Confirm a current-PID `[robotron-socket-wait]` line for every actor (slot 0 logs
to the supervisor output; other slots log to `logs/mame_instance_N.log`). Native
and Lua tests plus isolated real-MAME packet tests cover full/partial/legacy
messages and timeouts. At 100 microseconds the isolated waiting-thread CPU cost
fell about 97.2%; production gains depend on actor count. Paired evidence and
independent review are in `run-05/select-wait-embedded-summary.json` and
`run-05/select-wait-independent-review.json` under the campaign directory.
The initial 2026-10-15 trials selected 128 actors at 1000 microseconds. Initial
interval measurements produced about 66,040 samples/second and 61% GPU utilization,
versus 35,233 and 37% for 32 actors with busy polling. The 192-actor pilot yielded
only about 67,531 samples/second and averaged 7.5 samples per frame against the
configured cap of 8. These are sequential trials with a changing policy, not a
controlled timing benchmark. Later 2026-10-16 trials below use a different main
inference implementation and select 192 actors. Frozen policy comparisons continue to use exactly 16 actors.

`tools/run_frozen_sidecar.py` can evaluate an immutable checkpoint on the RTX PRO
2000 while GB300 training continues. It constructs an inference-only actor with
no optimizer or replay, owns 16 MAME processes on slots 4096–4111/port 9999, and
restores initialized boot assets into separate writable directories for every
actor. An empty NVRAM directory leaves this MAME build at its operator screen;
use the verified `sidecar-boot-assets-v1` snapshot recorded in the campaign.
The default command only prints a plan; `--launch` starts the private fleet.
Never use the global `startmame.sh` restart/kill commands during a private job.

The optional `--inference-backend inductor` preserves FP32 weights/inputs and
production TF32 settings, compiles before opening the listener, warms every
batch size 1–16 plus a serving thread, and rejects later recompilation, fallback,
or inference errors. Eager remains the default. A bounded GPU0 benchmark using
the protected 8M checkpoint measured populated action batches at 6.2–6.3 ms
eager versus 2.2–2.4 ms compiled, including CPU input and action readback, after
35.4 seconds of cold warmup. All 128 unrestricted greedy choices agreed, but
7/1152 held-fire choices differed and Q values failed a strict 1e-3 comparison.
Consequently each backend/device needs its own fresh incumbent reference and
matching candidate cohorts. A valid compiled GPU0 reference of the protected 8M
policy scored 73,804 points over 173 complete games. The saved 16M policy then
scored 96,885 and 103,580 across independent 144- and 133-game cohorts, with every
started game completed and all 16 private slots represented. Its descriptive
pooled mean is 100,100 (277 games; median 105,475; SE 2,098), 35.6% above its own
compiled GPU0 8M reference. All 16 actor-slot means improved. Game and paired-slot
bootstrap ratio sensitivities were [1.256, 1.474] and [1.302, 1.411]; repeated looks
and sequential runs prevent treating them as formal confirmatory confidence.
The exact policy is protected at
`models_dqn/managed-incumbents/fresh3-16M-confirmed.pt`; source/protected SHA256 is
`b8db175280b8e5d49865d14c83dccfe90737dfcb33d13d219ff5bb38d9abf281`.
See `logs/managed-learning-20261015/sidecar-fresh3-16M-confirmed-campaign.json`.
This GPU0 compiled series is separate from the historical GB300 score curve;
there is no direct 16M-versus-original gain estimate across those backends.
The progress figure and its validated input data are
`logs/managed-learning-20261015/learning-and-throughput-progress.{png,json}`;
`tools/render_learning_progress.py` reproduces the PNG.

Private receipts retain checkpoint/source/boot hashes, saved policy clocks,
actor ownership, readiness evidence, and runtime/cleanup timeout counts.
Every admitted whole game must finish and all 16 actors must be represented.
The official full-leg validator's 50-whole-game validity floor remains unchanged.
The 128M plan adds no extra publication count floor: retain the original whole-game
denominator and report low N as a precision caveat. Runtime timeouts invalidate
a leg, including
ambiguous or late-flushed events; timeouts caused solely by intentional shutdown
are separate diagnostics. Use a sufficiently long admission window (currently
600 seconds with a 1200-second total deadline). Never promote a cohort that fails its precommitted
validity or quality rules. Preserve the original receipt even when diagnosing a failure.

Transport recovery now closes Lua's connection on an action timeout and returns
`socket_ok=false`. Since action replies have no request ID, reusing that stream
could apply an old action to a later state or lose byte alignment. Python closes
on partial header/payload read timeout while retaining harmless zero-byte idle
polls. Its batcher uses one bounded request deadline, never duplicates a timed-out
batch request through solo inference, and propagates inference/stop/malformed
output errors instead of returning fictitious neutral success. The configured
inference deadline is 3000ms across queue admission and completion: strict 250ms
caused frequent reconnects with 128 actors. This leaves nominal 500ms headroom
below Lua's 3500ms whole-roundtrip timeout; parsing, replay enqueue, and transport
also consume time. Explicit shorter batcher deadlines remain supported. Expiry
still closes the client, with no solo retry. These fixes correct demonstrated
protocol defects; they do not establish the cause of longer throughput phases.
The confirmed GPU0 8M/16M cohorts above predate this source change and remain a
separate historical segment. A fresh fixed-transport GPU0 compiled reference of
the same protected 16M policy scored 95,474 across 133 complete games (median
95,125; SE 2,857). The saved 32M policy then passed two independent full-tail
cohorts of 123 and 113 games. Its descriptive pool is 121,245 over 236 games
(median 124,125; SE 2,508), 26.99% above that fresh fixed-transport 16M reference.
Both separate legs passed the stated mean gain of at least 5% and median at least
the reference; all 16 pooled actor-slot mean contrasts were positive. Game and
paired-slot bootstrap ratio sensitivities were [1.184, 1.363] and [1.206, 1.338],
with the same repeated-look and sequential-run limits described above.
The policy is protected at
`models_dqn/managed-incumbents/fresh3-32M-confirmed.pt`, SHA256
`50a374d33250713ba205024f85aaed3e50b62db913c29b2c2fb8c28392f051af`;
`logs/managed-learning-20261015/sidecar-fresh3-32M-confirmed-campaign.json` records
the exact source/boot/backend/checkpoint identities, saved clocks, and all tails.
The progress figure shows this fixed-transport 16M-to-32M-to-64M-to-128M-to-192M series separately from
the earlier GPU0 and GB300 curves. It does not connect the earlier 16M mean of
100,100 to the 32M point or infer a cross-segment gain.
CPU regression evidence and exact applied hashes are recorded in
`logs/managed-learning-20261015/run-06/socket-recovery-applied.json`.

The saved 64M policy passed another two independent full-tail GPU0 compiled
cohorts on the same fixed-transport/3s protocol: 110 and 107 complete games,
with means 144,405 and 160,058. Each leg independently exceeded the pooled
32M mean by at least 5% and its median of 124,125. The descriptive 217-game
pool has mean 152,123, median 146,575 and SE 3,283: +25.47% versus the two
separately valid 32M cohorts pooled above. All started games, including late
tails, completed; all 16 private slots were represented. Game and paired-slot
bootstrap ratio sensitivities were [1.184, 1.330] and [1.207, 1.306]. These
retain the same sequential-selection and host-pacing caveats, not formal
confirmatory confidence. The policy is protected at
`models_dqn/managed-incumbents/fresh3-64M-confirmed.pt`, SHA256
`fe88785bf9475c50a7a3df6cd04ab52caf46b706a7f6e22c36b99c21bcc93244`;
`logs/managed-learning-20261015/sidecar-fresh3-64M-confirmed-campaign.json`
records both real receipts, saved policy clocks and unchanged private backend.

Later efficiency evidence is dated 2026-10-16 UTC and uses fixed first-180s
counter windows after settling. The 192-actor run13 reference at
07:15:49.799–07:18:49.971 measured 64,825 samples/s. Run14 with native priority
updates at 07:27:46.891–07:30:46.907 measured 66,918 samples/s: +3.23% against
that recent reference (+1.31% against the older 66,052 reference), below both
5% gain gates. Native execution counters confirmed successes with no fallback
or native error in these windows. The 256-actor trial at
07:34:05.934–07:37:06.048 fell to 60,537 samples/s (−9.54% versus native192)
while samples/frame remained about 8; it was rejected and 192 restored. The
main layout at this dated observation uses exact-size CUDA graphs, a 1ms
collector wait and 1000µs native socket readiness. These sequential operational
trials are not randomized same-policy tests or proof that every rate change
was caused by the tested implementation. Evidence is in
`logs/managed-learning-20261015/run-14/nativebatch-fleet-pilot-campaign.json`.

The performance windows above had no main inference-deadline errors. Later,
six main-only deadline errors clustered at 07:55:40.206 UTC, with affected
connections closing and reconnecting; the entire live run is therefore not
error-free. They were outside those performance windows and neither private
64M quality cohort had a runtime timeout. Main graph/native fatal and fallback
counters remained zero in the recorded observation. The nearby archive/save
activity is correlation, not an established cause. See
`run-14/sidecar-64M-confirm01-main-deadline-observation.json` under the campaign
directory. Host inference/refresh telemetry is documented in
`tools/PIPELINE_REPORTING.md`; its timers are host elapsed time, not GPU kernel
time, and its CPU tests do not establish controlled live instrumentation overhead.

The selected learner batch is now 4096 after a completed sequential A–B–A trial
on 2026-10-16 UTC. All legs retained 192 actors, exact inference graphs, direct
pinned replay fill, native priority updates, 1ms collector wait, 1000µs socket
readiness and replay cap 8. Each primary window followed 90 seconds of settling:

| Chronological leg | UTC window | Batch | Samples/s | Counter FPS | GPU-only samples/J |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| A, run16 | 08:24:31.463–08:27:31.477 | 2048 | 66,463.74 | 8,314.19 | 160.85 |
| B, run17 | 08:34:44.489–08:37:44.504 | 4096 | 71,332.63 | 8,913.69 | 166.70 |
| A return, run18 | 08:43:02.112–08:46:02.127 | 2048 | 67,521.54 | 8,431.75 | 161.90 |

B exceeded the stronger A return by 5.644%, clearing the prespecified
`1.05 * max(A, Areturn)` gate of 70,897.615 samples/s. All three recorded runtime,
actor, graph/native and replay-pressure guards passed; the windows had no new
deadline errors, native/graph fallback, nonfinite/training errors or replay
drops. This statement applies to these three fixed windows, not the entire
training campaign. The independent decision and full summaries are linked by
`logs/managed-learning-20261015/run-18/batch2048-4096-ABA-independent-decision.json`.
Rates use raw sample/frame counter deltas over the recorded observer-UTC
intervals, rather than console-smoothed FPS. Energy integrates sampled GPU
power only and excludes CPU/system power.

This selects throughput, not improved learning quality. Adam advances once per
batch, so 4096 halves optimizer updates per processed sample; diagnostic cadence
20 also halves diagnostics per sample. LR and imitation schedules retain their
sample clock (`optimized_samples / 512`) with no LR scaling. The hard target
period stays 512,000 samples, while actor refresh quantizes from 26,624 to
28,672 samples. The sequential runs evolve policy/replay/RNG trajectories and
do not establish learning equivalence or formal statistical confidence. The
quality assessments and their precommitted gates remain separate from this
throughput selection.
The progress figure retains the three quality segments unchanged and now shows
this batch chronology in panel D; historical actor-scaling trial data remain in
its JSON and archived earlier figure. No new run19 performance result is claimed.

The subsequent training-compiler A–B–A trial on 2026-10-16 retained batch 4096,
192 actors and the existing exact actor graphs. A used `DQN_COMPILE_MODE=default`;
For B, this project's `max-autotune-no-cudagraphs` prefix mapped to
`max_autotune=True` for the training compile options; it did not invoke the
entire PyTorch named preset. Training CUDA graphs remained disabled and the
actor backend was unchanged.
The fixed 180-second windows were:

| Leg | UTC window | Samples/s | Outcome |
| --- | --- | ---: | --- |
| Default A, run20 | 10:51:00.326–10:54:00.329 | 70,927.95 | Clean baseline |
| Autotune B, run21 | 11:10:51.813–11:13:51.827 | 71,355.98 | INVALID; retained raw rate only |
| Default A return, run22 | 11:21:58.252–11:24:58.268 | 69,261.72 | Clean return |

B failed the precommitted nonfinite-gradient guard: an Inf gradient-norm reading
was retained with finite loss. AMP outcome counters were not deployed in B, so
its optimizer application/skip status and a compiler cause are unproven. The
invalid leg is excluded from throughput selection; default compilation remains
selected. The precommitted gate also required a valid B to reach
`1.05 * max(A, Areturn)`, or 74,474.35 samples/s. B's retained raw rate was only
0.6035% above the stronger A, below that gate; this is descriptive arithmetic,
not a rescue or selection of the invalid leg. Later exposure telemetry does not rescue or reclassify that leg.
Sources are `compiler-default-A-independent-summary.json` in run20,
`compiler-B-independent-summary.json` and
`compiler-B-final-guard-provenance-audit.json` in run21, and
`compiler-Areturn-independent-summary.json` and
`compiler-Areturn-final-independent-audit.json` in run22, under
`logs/managed-learning-20261015`. Sequential policy/replay/kernel trajectories
preclude a controlled learning-quality or causal speed comparison.
